\displaystyle e^{-i{\rm m}(x^{0}+i\Theta^{A}\bar{\Theta}_{A})}\left(\phi_{{\rm m}}+\Theta^{A}\psi_{A{\rm m}}+\frac{1}{2}\Theta^{B}\Theta^{A}\phi_{AB{\rm m}}+\frac{1}{3!}\Theta^{C}\Theta^{B}\Theta^{A}\psi_{ABC{\rm m}}+\right.\qquad

(5.82)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4!}\Theta^{D}\Theta^{C}\Theta^{B}\Theta^{A}\psi_{ABCD{\rm m}}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{5!\,3!}\epsilon_{A_{1}\ldots A_{5}B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}}\Theta^{A_{5}}\ldots\Theta^{A_{1}}\tilde{\psi}{}^{B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}}_{{\rm m}}+\frac{1}{6!\,2!}\epsilon_{A_{1}\ldots A_{4}B_{1}B_{2}}\Theta^{A_{6}}\ldots\Theta^{A_{1}}\tilde{\phi}{}^{B_{1}B_{2}{\rm m}}_{{\rm m}}+\qquad

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{7!}\epsilon_{A_{1}\ldots A_{7}B}\Theta^{A_{7}}\ldots\Theta^{A_{1}}\tilde{\psi}{}_{{\rm m}}^{B}+\frac{1}{8!}\epsilon_{A_{1}\ldots A_{8}}\Theta^{A_{8}}\ldots\Theta^{A_{1}}\tilde{\phi}_{{\rm m}}\right)\;\qquad

obeying, besides

\displaystyle\bar{\text{D}}{}^{A}\Xi_{{}_{{\text{D0}}}}=0\;,\qquad\bar{\text{D}}{}^{A}=\bar{\partial}{}^{A}+i\Theta^{A}\partial_{x^{0}}=\bar{\partial}{}^{A}\,e^{-i\Theta^{A}\bar{\Theta}_{A}\partial_{x^{0}}}=-({\text{D}}{}_{A})^{*}\;,\qquad

(5.83)

also

\displaystyle\text{D}_{A_{1}}\ldots\text{D}_{A_{4}}\Xi_{{}_{{\text{D0}}}}=\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon_{A_{1}\ldots A_{4}B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}}\bar{\text{D}}{}^{B_{1}}\ldots\bar{\text{D}}{}^{B_{4}}\bar{\Xi}_{{}_{{\text{D0}}}}\;,\qquad(\Xi_{{}_{{\text{D0}}}})^{*}=\bar{\Xi}_{{}_{{\text{D0}}}}\;.

(5.84)

with
(\Xi_{{}_{{\text{D0}}}})^{*}=\bar{\Xi}_{{}_{{\text{D0}}}}
and

\displaystyle{\text{D}}_{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\partial}_{A}+i\bar{\Theta}_{A}\partial_{x^{0}}={\partial}_{A}\,e^{i\Theta^{A}\bar{\Theta}_{A}\partial_{x^{0}}}=-(\bar{\text{D}}{}^{A})^{*}\;.\qquad

(5.85)

In (
5.82
)
{\rm m}
in the exponent is the mass of D
0
–brane. We also use subindex
{\rm m}
to distinguish the component of state vector superfield of D
0
-brane from that of the state vector of IIA supergraviton.

Like in the case of the linearized supergravity discussed above, the complex on-shell superfield essentially depends on
octuplet of complex fermionic coordinates
\Theta^{A}
, but not on its c.c. This fermionic coordinate carries the index of fundamental representation of ·
SU(8)
which was shown to be a hidden symmetry also of the quantum theory of D
0
brane

Bandos:2025pxv
. The complex fermionic coordinate function of D
0
brane is constructed from the standard real fermionic coordinates
\theta^{\alpha 1}
and
\theta_{\alpha}^{2}
of type IIA superspace by

\displaystyle\Theta^{A}=\Theta^{\underline{q}}w_{\underline{q}}^{A}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\theta^{\alpha 1}{{\rm v}}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}+\theta_{\alpha}^{2}{{\rm v}}_{\underline{q}}{}^{\alpha}){{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{A}\;=(\bar{\Theta}_{A})^{*}\;

(5.86)

using spinor moving frame variable of D
0
–brane

{{\rm v}}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}\in\text{Spin}(1,9)\;,\qquad\alpha=1,\ldots,16\;,\qquad{\underline{q}}=1,\ldots,16

(5.87)

and the suitable bridge variables given by
complex 16
\times 8
matrix
{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{A}=(\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A})^{*}
obeying

\displaystyle\bar{{{\rm w}}}_{\underline{q}A}\bar{{{\rm w}}}_{\underline{q}B}=0={{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{A}{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{B}\;,\qquad\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{B}=\delta_{A}{}^{B}\;,\qquad\bar{{{\rm w}}}_{\underline{q}A}{{\rm w}}_{\underline{p}}^{A}+{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{A}\bar{{{\rm w}}}_{\underline{p}A}=\delta_{qp}\;.\qquad

(5.88)

These relations imply that
({\rm w}_{\underline{q}}^{A}\,,\,\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A})
variables parametrize the
\text{SO}(16)
valued matrix

\left(\begin{matrix}\Re{\rm e}{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{A}\;|\;\Im{\rm m}{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}}^{A}\;\end{matrix}\right)\in\text{SO}(16)\;

(5.89)

The moving frame vectors of the D0–brane

\left({{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{0},{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{I}\right)\;\in\;SO(1,9)\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{0}{{\rm u}}^{\mu(0)}=1\;,\qquad{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{0}{{\rm u}}^{\mu I}=0\;,\qquad{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{I}{{\rm u}}^{\mu J}=\delta^{IJ}\;,\qquad

(5.90)

are constructed from the spinor moving frame matrix (
5.87
) as indicated by the constraints
Bandos:2000tg

\displaystyle{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{0}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}={{\rm v}}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}{{\rm v}}_{\beta}{}^{\underline{q}}\;,\qquad

(5.91)

\displaystyle{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{I}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}={{\rm v}}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}{{\rm v}}_{\beta}{}^{\underline{p}}\;,\qquad{{\rm v}}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}\tilde{\sigma}{}_{\mu}^{\alpha\beta}{{\rm v}}_{\beta}{}^{\underline{q}}={{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{0}\delta_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}+{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{I}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}\;,\qquad I=1,\ldots,9\;.\qquad

(5.92)

Here
\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}
are 9d Dirac matrices which are symmetric, traceless and obey the Clifford algebra,

\displaystyle\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}=\gamma^{I}_{\underline{p}\underline{q}}\;,\qquad\gamma^{I}_{\underline{p}\underline{p}}=0\;,\qquad\gamma^{I}\gamma^{J}+\gamma^{J}\gamma^{I}=2\delta^{IJ}{\mathbb{I}}_{16\times 16}\;.\qquad

(5.93)

The timelike momentum of the D0–brane is proportional to

{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{0}
,

p_{\mu}^{{D0}}={\rm m}{{\rm u}}_{\mu}^{0}\,\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad p_{\mu}^{{D0}}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}={\rm m}{\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}{\rm v}_{\beta}{}^{\underline{q}}

(5.94)

While the first of the constraints (
5.91
) is invariant under
SO(16)
symmetry acting on
\underline{q},\underline{p}
indices, the remaining constraints (
5.92
) break this down to
Spin(9)\subset SO(16)
which acts also on

({w}_{\underline{q}}^{\,A},\bar{w}_{\underline{q}A})
variables. These
provide a bridge between
Spin(9)
and
SU(8)
groups and can be called internal harmonic variables. The discussion on the degrees of freedom of the superparticle mechanics described in terms of all these variables can be found in
Bandos:2025pxv
.

Like in the case of type II massless superparticles, it is useful to construct the complex version of spinor frame variables/helicity spinors by contraction of spinor frame matrix with internal harmonics,

{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{A}={\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}{\rm w}_{\underline{q}}^{A}\;,\qquad{\bar{\rm v}}_{\alpha A}={\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}\;.\qquad

(5.95)

In the case of multiparticle amplitude involving, beside the massive, also massless particles, the above (spinor) moving frame variables of D
0
–brane can be decomposed on the same reference (spinor) moving frame as the (spinor) moving frame of massless superparicles are decomposed. In this case, in addition to Eqs. (
5.4
), (
5.5
),
we can consider

\displaystyle{\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}l_{q}^{+\underline{q}}+v_{\alpha\dot{p}}^{\;+}l_{\dot{p}}^{-\underline{q}}\;.\qquad

(5.96)

However, we have not found such a decomposition particularly useful. For the case of processes involving more than one massless superparticles, it turns out
to be more practical to use, following the
D=4
approach in
Arkani-Hamed:2017jhn
, the decomposition on helicity spinors of two of these, assuming that their momenta are not collinear,

\displaystyle{\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{\alpha q{\rm 1}}^{\;-}l_{q{\rm 1}}^{+\underline{q}}+v_{\alpha{q}{\rm 2}}^{\;-}l_{{q}{\rm 2}}^{+\underline{q}}\;.\qquad

(5.97)

We will use such a decomposition below in our discussion on three point amplitude which involves
one D
0
-brane.

The quantum state spectrum of D
0
–brane was shown to be the massive counterpart of type IIA supergravity multiplet
Bandos:2025pxv
formed by a massive graviton, massive gravitino and massive 3-form fields. The solution of (the Fourier image of) the linearized equations can be written in terms of the moving frame and spinor moving frame variables
described above by

Bandos:2025pxv

\displaystyle h_{\mu\nu}={\rm u}_{\mu}^{I}{\rm u}_{\nu}^{J}h^{IJ}\;,\qquad h^{IJ}=h^{JI}=h^{(IJ)}\;,\qquad h^{II}=0\;,\qquad

(5.98)

\displaystyle A_{\mu\nu\rho}={\rm u}_{\mu}^{I}{\rm u}_{\nu}^{J}{\rm u}_{\rho}^{K}A^{IJK}\;,\qquad A^{IJK}=A^{[IJK]}\;,\qquad

(5.99)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mu}^{\alpha 1}={\rm v}_{\underline{q}}^{\alpha}\Psi^{I}_{\underline{q}}\;,\qquad\Psi_{\mu\alpha}^{\;2}={\rm v}^{\underline{q}}_{\alpha}\Psi^{I}_{\underline{q}}\;,\qquad\gamma^{I}_{qp}\Psi^{I}_{p}=0\;,\qquad

(5.100)

where
p_{\mu}={\rm m}{\rm u}_{\mu}^{{\rm 0}}
is assumed.

The expressions for bosonic
h^{IJ}
,
A^{IJK}
and fermionic
\Psi^{I}_{\underline{q}}
in terms of the components of the on-shell superfield can be found in
Bandos:2025pxv
.
One can also express the components of the on-shell superfield in terms of
h^{IJ}
,
A^{IJK}
and
\Psi^{I}_{\underline{q}}
. In particular one can find

23
23

23

The relative coefficients for the terms in this expression are not essential for our discussion below and are set to unity for simplicity. This is to say, we did not try to fix them by correspondence with ’inverse’ expressions in
Bandos:2025pxv
which themselves are indicative showing the correspondence between degrees of freedom.

\displaystyle\phi_{ABCD\;{\rm m}}=h^{IJ}\left(U_{I}^{\check{I}}U_{J}^{\check{J}}t^{\check{I}\check{J}}_{ABCD}+U_{J}\bar{U}_{J}\sigma^{\check{I}}{}_{[AB}\sigma^{\check{J}}{}_{CD]}\right)+A^{IJK}\left(U_{I}^{\check{I}}U_{J}\bar{U}_{K}\;it^{\check{I}}_{ABCD}+U_{I}^{\check{I}}U_{J}^{\check{J}}U_{K}^{\check{K}}\;it^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}}_{ABCD}\right)\;,\qquad

(5.101)

where

U_{I}{}^{\check{K}}
are six real, mutually orthogonal unit vectors vectors
U_{I}=(\bar{U}_{I})^{*}
is complex null vector orthogonal to the above vectors and normalized by

U_{I}\bar{U}_{I}=2
. Resuming;

\displaystyle U_{I}U_{I}=0=\bar{U}_{I}\bar{U}_{I}\;,\qquad U_{I}\bar{U}_{I}=2\;,\qquad U_{I}U_{I}{}^{\check{J}}=0=\bar{U}_{I}U_{I}{}^{\check{J}}\;,\qquad U_{I}{}^{\check{J}}U_{I}{}^{\check{K}}=\delta^{\check{J}\check{K}}\;\qquad

(5.102)

which implies that these vectors form the
SO(9)
valued ”internal frame matrix”

\displaystyle U_{I}^{(J)}=\left(U_{I}{}^{\check{J}},U_{I}{}^{(8)},U_{I}{}^{(9)}\right)=\left(U_{I}{}^{\check{J}},\frac{1}{2}\left(U_{I}+\bar{U}_{I}\right),\frac{1}{2i}\left(U_{I}-\bar{U}_{I}\right)\right)\;\in\;SO(9)\;.\qquad

(5.103)

Following
Bandos:2017eof
, we can define the square root of the above spinor frame variables as
Spin(9)
valued matrix constrained by the condition of the invariance of the generalized
SO(9)
Pauli matrices
\gamma^{I}_{pq}
. Actually such variables are provided by the above introduced
{\rm w}_{\underline{q}}^{A}
and
\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}=({\rm w}_{\underline{q}}^{A})^{*}
after we impose on them, besides (
5.88
) also the above mentioned constraints.
We however prefer to impose on them a bit more general constraints

\displaystyle U\!\!\!\!/{}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}:=U_{I}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}=2\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}\bar{{\cal U}}{}^{AB}\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{p}B}\;,\qquad\bar{U}{}\!\!\!\!/{}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}:=\bar{U}{}_{I}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}=2{\rm w}_{\underline{q}}^{A}{{\cal U}}_{AB}{\rm w}_{\underline{p}}^{B}\;,\qquad

(5.104)

\displaystyle U\!\!\!\!/{}^{\check{J}}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}:=U_{I}^{\check{J}}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}=i{\rm w}_{\underline{q}}^{A}(\sigma^{\check{J}}\bar{{\cal U}}){}_{A}{}^{B}\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{p}B}+i\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}(\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{J}}{\cal U})^{A}{}_{B}{\rm w}_{\underline{p}}^{B}\;,\qquad

(5.105)

\displaystyle\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{p}B}=U_{I}{\cal U}_{AB}\;,\qquad{\rm w}_{\underline{q}}^{A}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}{\rm w}_{\underline{p}}^{B}=\bar{U}_{I}\bar{{\cal U}}^{AB}\;,\qquad\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}\gamma^{I}_{\underline{q}\underline{p}}{\rm w}_{\underline{p}}^{B}=iU_{I}^{\check{J}}(\sigma^{\check{J}}\bar{{\cal U}})_{A}{}^{B}\;.\qquad

(5.106)

These include the symmetric unitary complex matrix
{\cal U}_{AB}=(\bar{{\cal U}}{}^{AB})^{*}
parametrizing the coset
SU(8)/SO(7)
and complex matrices

\sigma^{\check{J}}_{AB}=(\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{J}BA})^{*}
which are obtained from
SO(7)
Clebsch-Gordan coefficients by
SU(8)
rotation
(see Appendix
C
for details).

V.5.2
Three particle kinematics and on-shell amplitudes of type IIA string theory involving D0 brane

Let us consider the case of scattering of three (super)particles,
two massless and two massive.
Namely,
we will be interested in amplitudes and superamplitudes for the process involving two type IIA supergravity (
{\rm i}={\rm 1,2}
) and one D0–brane supermultiplet (
{\rm i}={\rm 3}
). The basic bra-ket notation for spinor helicity variables and polarization spinors are

\displaystyle<\,{\rm 1}^{-}_{q}\,|=v_{\alpha q\,{\rm 1}}^{-}\;,\qquad<\,{\rm 2}^{-}_{q}\,|=v_{\alpha q\,{\rm 2}}^{-}\;,\qquad<\,{\rm 3}^{\,\underline{q}}\,|={\rm v}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}^{\;\;\underline{q}}\;,\qquad

(5.107)

\displaystyle|\,{\rm 1}^{-}_{\dot{q}}\,>=\,v_{\dot{p}{\rm 1}}^{-\alpha}\;,\qquad|\,{\rm 2}^{-}_{\dot{q}}\,>=\,v_{\dot{p}{\rm 2}}^{-\alpha}\;,\qquad|\,{\rm 3}_{\underline{q}}\,>=\,{\rm v}_{\underline{q}{\rm 3}}{}^{\alpha}\;.\qquad

(5.108)

The contractions of the helicity spinors corresponding to massless states
{\rm 1}
and
{\rm 2}
,
<\,{\rm 1}^{-}_{q}\,|\,{\rm 2}^{-}_{\dot{q}}\,>
and
<\,{\rm 2}^{-}_{q}\,|\,{\rm 1}^{-}_{\dot{q}}\,>
, are determined by (
5.9
). To specify the contractions involving helicity spinors of a massive state
{\rm 3}
we have to use the decomposition (
5.97
) and the momentum conservation (
5.30
) which in this case is solved by

u_{\mu{\rm 3}}^{\,0}=-\frac{1}{m}\left(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}u_{\mu{\rm 1}}^{\,=}+\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}}u_{\mu{\rm 2}}^{\,=}\right)\qquad\Leftarrow\qquad 0=p_{\mu{\rm 1}}+p_{\mu{\rm 2}}+p_{\mu{\rm 3}}\;.

(5.109)

Below we will conventionally skip subindex
{\rm 3}
as we consider a process with only one massive particle in this section, and also use the notation

\displaystyle l^{\#}_{{\rm i}}=-\frac{\rho^{\#}_{{\rm i}}}{m}\;.

(5.110)

The decomposition (
5.97
)
of helicity spinors of the D
0
–brane in terms of helicity spinors of two non-colinear gravitons can always be equivalently written in the form

\displaystyle{\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2l_{{\rm 1}}^{\#}}v_{\alpha q{\rm 1}}^{\;-}l_{q{\rm 1}}^{\;\underline{q}}+\sqrt{2l_{{\rm 2}}^{\#}}v_{\alpha{q}{\rm 2}}^{\;-}l_{{q}{\rm 2}}^{\;\underline{q}}\;\qquad

(5.111)

However, the nontrivial statement is that in this expression

l_{{\rm 1}q}^{\;\underline{q}}
and
l_{{\rm 2}q}^{\;\underline{q}}
obey

\displaystyle l_{{\rm 1}q}^{\;\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 1}p}^{\;\underline{q}}=\delta_{qp}\;,\qquad{}\qquad l_{{\rm 1}q}^{\;\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{\;\underline{q}}=0\;,\qquad{}\qquad l_{{\rm 2}q}^{\;\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{\;\underline{q}}=\delta_{qp}\;\qquad

(5.112)

and hence form the
SO(16)
valued matrix,

\left(\,l_{{\rm 1}q}^{\;\underline{q}}\,,\,l_{{\rm 2}q}^{\;\underline{q}}\right)\;\in\;O(16)
.
This is shown in Appendix
E
. The expression for the complex version of D
0
–brane spinor moving frame variables
(
5.95
)
can be easily obtained from (
5.111
).

V.5.3
On on-shell amplitudes of type IIA string theory involving D0 brane.

Let us consider a ’massive’ counterpart of the three amplitude
(
5.56
), namely
A(\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 1}}\,\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 2}}\,\phi_{ABCD{\rm m}})
corresponding to the scattering of massive particle described by the intermediate
(
\propto\Theta^{\wedge 4}_{{\rm 3}}
)
component of the on–shell superfield of
D
0
–brane and of two real parts of the leading (
\Theta^{-}_{{\rm 1,2}}
–independent) components of the on-shell superfields that describe type IIA supergravity multiplets. As suggested by Eq. (
5.101
), it should contain some of the contributions proportional to the amplitudes

A({\phi}^{IIA}_{{\rm 1}}\,{\phi}^{IIA}_{{\rm 2}}\,h^{IJ}_{{\rm m}})

and
A({\phi}^{IIA}_{{\rm 1}}\,{\phi}^{IIA}_{{\rm 2}}\,\,A^{IJK}_{{\rm m}})
.

These amplitudes should be constructed from the light-like momenta of scalar massless particles (dilatons),
p_{\mu{\rm 1}}
and
p_{\mu{\rm 2}}
, and the polarization vector of the massive counterpart of graviton and 3-form gauge field which should be constructed from the orthogonal vectors of the D
0
-brane moving frame (
5.90
),
{\rm u}_{\mu{\rm 3}}^{I}={\rm u}_{\mu}^{I}
. As the index structure of
A^{IJK}_{{\rm m}}
cannot be reproduced using the contractions of these three ingredients on their 10-vector indices, we conclude that only
A(\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 1}}\,\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 2}}\,h^{IJ}_{{\rm m}})
contributes to
the amplitude
A(\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 1}}\,\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 2}}\,\phi_{ABCD{\rm m}})
which then reads
(
cf.
(
V.3
))
24
24

24

The relative coefficients make the expression in the brackets traceless in its SO(9) vector indices
I
and
J
which corresponds to tracelessness of
h^{IJ}
.

\displaystyle A(\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 1}}\,\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 2}}\,\phi_{ABCD{\rm m}})=p_{{\rm 1}\mu}{\rm u}^{\mu I}_{{\rm 3}}p_{{\rm 2}\nu}{\rm u}^{\nu J}_{{\rm 3}}\,\left(U_{I}^{\check{I}}U_{J}^{\check{J}}t^{\check{I}\check{J}}_{ABCD}+\frac{1}{2}U_{(I}\bar{U}_{J)}\sigma^{\check{K}}{}_{[AB}\sigma^{\check{K}}{}_{CD]}\right)

(5.113)

Using our result on three particle kinematics, after a straightforward but not short calculations (described in Appendix
E.2
) we have obtained the following expression for
this amplitude

\displaystyle A(\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 1}}\,\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 2}}\,\phi_{ABCD{\rm m}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{2}}{16{\rm m}^{2}}\,|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|^{4}\left((l_{q{\rm 1}}{\rm w}^{E})\,(l_{q{\rm 1}}\bar{{\rm w}}_{F})\,(\sigma^{(\check{I}|}{\bar{\cal U}})_{E}{}^{F}\;(l_{p{\rm 1}}{\rm w}^{G})\,(l_{p{\rm 1}}\bar{{\rm w}}_{H})\,(\sigma^{|\check{J})}{\bar{\cal U}})_{G}{}^{H}t^{\check{I}\check{J}}_{ABCD}-\right.

(5.114)

\displaystyle{}\hskip 113.81102pt\left.-\frac{1}{8}\left|(l_{q{\rm 1}}\bar{{\rm w}}_{E}){\bar{\cal U}}{}^{EF}(l_{q{\rm 1}}\bar{{\rm w}}_{F})\;\right|^{2}\sigma^{\check{K}}{}_{[AB}\sigma^{\check{K}}{}_{CD]}\right)\;.

In it the contracted
\underline{q},\underline{p}=1,..,16
indices are not written explicitly.

It is natural to try to use this ampitude
as a reference point for finding the superamplitude in a manner similar to that we followed for the case of process with three type II supergravity multiplets. However, as we are going to show,
there is an issue that
hampers such a straightforward way.

V.5.4
Problems on the way towards on-shell superamplitudes of type IIA string theory involving D0 brane

By analogy with the massless case, we can expect that amplitude (
5.114
) can be obtained from superamplitude by applying Grassmann integrations or a product of fermionic derivatives as (
cf.
(
5.58
))

\displaystyle A(\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 1}}\,\Re{\rm{e}}{\phi}_{{\rm 2}}\,\phi_{ABCD{\rm m}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\propto\left(\int d^{4}(\Theta_{{\rm 3}})_{ABCD}\,{\cal A}_{3\;00{\rm m}}\right)|_{\Theta^{-}_{{\rm 1}}=0=\Theta^{-}_{{\rm 2}},\Theta_{{\rm 3}}=0}

(5.115)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\propto\left(D_{A\,{\rm 3}}D_{B\,{\rm 3}}D_{C\,{\rm 3}}D_{D\,{\rm 3}}\,{\cal A}_{3\;00{\rm m}}\right)|_{\Theta^{-}_{{\rm 1}}=0=\Theta^{-}_{{\rm 2}},\Theta_{{\rm 3}}=0}\;.

The problem to be solved is then to find the expression for
the 3-point superamplitude with two type IIA supergravity multiplets and a D
0
-brane supermultiplet (roughly speaking, with two massless and one massive supergravitons),
which we have denoted by
{\cal A}_{3\;00{\rm m}}
.

The simple prescription which was elaborated for type IIB case and, as we have shown, allowing also for the description of scattering of type IIA supergravitons, meets in this case some difficulty which can be followed to difference of
massive and massless representations of the supersymmetry algebra.

If we define the complex supercharges of massive superparticle with momentum (
5.94
) by the first expressions in Eqs. (
5.77
) and (
5.78
), their non vanishing anticommutator will be given by

\displaystyle\{{\hbox{\mybb Q}}_{\alpha{\rm 3}},\bar{{\hbox{\mybb Q}}}_{\beta{\rm 3}}\}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-i{\rm m}\,{\rm u}_{\mu{\rm 3}}^{0}\,\left(\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}+k\!\!\!/{}_{\alpha\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\mu\gamma\delta}k\!\!\!/{}_{\delta\beta}\right)\,=\,-2i{\rm m}\,{\rm u}_{\mu{\rm 3}}^{0}\,\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}\,

(5.116)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-i{\rm m}\,\left({\rm v}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}{}^{\underline{q}}{\rm v}_{\beta{\rm 3}}{}^{\underline{q}}+k\!\!\!/{}_{\alpha\gamma}{\rm v}_{\underline{q}}^{\gamma}\,k\!\!\!/{}_{\beta\delta}{\rm v}_{\underline{q}}^{\delta}\right)=-2i{\rm m}\,{\rm v}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}{}^{\underline{q}}{\rm v}_{\beta{\rm 3}}{}^{\underline{q}}=-4i{\rm m}\,{\rm v}_{{\rm 3}(\alpha}{}^{A}\bar{{\rm v}}_{\beta){\rm 3}A}\;.

(5.117)

The first form of this equation, (
5.116
), is valid provided
k_{\mu}
is spacelike unit vector orthogonal to
{\rm u}_{\mu{\rm 3}}^{0}
, i.e.

k^{\mu}{\rm u}_{\mu{\rm 3}}^{0}=0
.
Using the momentum conservation conditions
(
5.109
), in the gauge (
5.6
)–(
5.8
) it is easy to check that this is indeed the case for
k_{\mu}
related with T-duality direction and thus obeying (
5.68
) for
{\rm i}=1,2
.
(Eq. (
5.80
) and (
D.51
) can be also used).

The problem comes from the final expression in (
5.117
), namely from symmetrization over spinor indices in it. This originates in the form of the unity decomposition in the last equation of (
5.88
)
characteristic for the case of massive superparticle. This term cannot be reproduced if we realize say
{\hbox{\mybb Q}}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}
as multiplication operator

\bar{{\rm v}}_{\alpha A}\Theta^{A}:={\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}A}\Theta^{A}
and
\bar{{\hbox{\mybb Q}}}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}
by differential operator
with respect to the fermionic coordinate,

{\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{A}\partial_{A}:={\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}{\rm w}_{\underline{q}}{}^{A}\frac{\partial}{\partial\Theta^{A}}
. Indeed, in this case the r.h.s. of the anticommutator would contain
\bar{{\rm v}}_{\alpha A}{\rm v}_{\beta}{}^{A}
the antisymmetric part of which,

\bar{v}_{[\alpha|A}v_{|\beta]}{}^{A}={\rm v}_{\alpha}{}^{\underline{q}}{\rm v}_{\beta}{}^{\underline{p}}{\rm w}_{[\underline{p}}{}^{A}\bar{{\rm w}}_{\underline{q}]A}
, is generically nonvanishing.

It is instructive to take a look on this problem from a bit different side. To this end, let us write the representation for original ’real’ supersymmetry generators in our complex reduced configuration space. The action of supersymmetry on the original
\theta^{\alpha 1}
and
\theta_{\alpha}^{2}
coordinates as well as the supersymemtry (super)algebra allows us to find

\displaystyle{\hbox{\mybb Q}}_{\alpha\;{\rm 3}}^{\;1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left({\rm v}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}{}^{A}\frac{\partial}{\partial\Theta^{A}_{{\rm 3}}}+4{\rm m}\bar{{\rm v}}_{\alpha A{\rm 3}}\Theta^{A}_{{\rm 3}}\right)\;,\qquad{\hbox{\mybb Q}}^{\alpha 2}_{{\rm 3}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left({\rm v}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}{}^{A}\frac{\partial}{\partial\Theta^{A}_{{\rm 3}}}+4{\rm m}\bar{{\rm v}}{}^{\alpha}_{A{\rm 3}}\Theta^{A}_{{\rm 3}}\right)\;,\qquad

(5.118)

It is not difficult to observe that

{\hbox{\mybb Q}}^{\alpha 2}_{{\rm 3}}={\rm u}^{0}_{\mu}\tilde{\sigma}^{\mu\alpha\beta}{\hbox{\mybb Q}}^{1}_{\beta{\rm 3}}

(5.119)

which implies that the action of the
type IIA supersymmetry on the invariant subsuperspace defined through the use of analytical basis (see
Bandos:2025pxv
) is not faithful.
This is not the case for supergravity multiplet described by quantum massless superparicle since no counterparts of
the relation (
5.119
) exists in this case.

The consequence of the above observation is that the complex supercharge and its conjugate, defined with the use of T-duality related complex structure as in (
5.78
) and (
5.77
) in the massive case are expressed algebraically through the same real supercharge,

\displaystyle\bar{{\hbox{\mybb Q}}}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(Q_{\alpha 1,{\rm 3}}-ik\!\!\!/{}_{\alpha\beta}{\hbox{\mybb Q}}_{2,{\rm 3}}^{\beta})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}-i(k\!\!\!/{}\sigma^{\mu})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta})Q_{\beta 1,{\rm 3}}\;,\qquad

(5.120)

\displaystyle{\hbox{\mybb Q}}_{\alpha{\rm 3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(Q_{\alpha 1,{\rm 3}}+ik\!\!\!/{}_{\alpha\beta}{\hbox{\mybb Q}}_{2,{\rm 3}}^{\beta})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}+i(k\!\!\!/{}\sigma^{\mu})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta})Q_{\beta 1,{\rm 3}}\;,\qquad

(5.121)

and thus both include the sum of algebraic expression and differential operator.

The above discussed issues make solution of the supersymmetric Ward identities for superamplitude involving one massive and two massless
superparticles more complicated than it was in the purely massless
case discussed in the previous subsections. Probably this is the reason why superamplitudes for massive supermultiplets have been addressed only recently and elaborated only for
D=4

{\cal N}=1
case
Herderschee:2019ofc
;
Delgado:2025oev
.
We plan to address this problem for 10D superamplitude in forthcoming paper(s).

VI
Conclusion and discussion

In this paper we firstly
performed covariant quantization of 10D type IIB superparticle in such a manner that made manifest the hidden SU(8) symmetry of linearized type IIB supergravity which describes its quantum state spectrum. Then we
quantizatized in a similar way type IIA superparticle and discussed its peculiarities as well as the appearance of the hidden
SU(8)
symmetry. Finally we discussed the generalization of the on-shell superfield formalism obtained as a result of the above mentioned superparticle quantization to superamplitudes, made
some observations on their properties, and pointed
out some problems to be solved.

The hidden
SU(8)
symmetry of type II supergravities found in this paper is of interest because
SU(8)
is R–symmetry of
D=4

{\cal N}=8
supersymmetry algebra and also a local symmetry of the maximal D=4
{\cal N}=8
supergravity which can be obtained from
10D
type IIB one by dimensional reduction.
Actually the
D=4

{\cal N}=8
supergravity was obtained in
Cremmer:1979up
by dimensional reduction of 11D supergravity
Cremmer:1978km
supplemented by dualization of antisymmetric tensor fields to scalars. After this last stage the scalar fields of the maximal supergravity multiplet was found to parametrize the coset
{\hbox{\mybb E}}_{7,+7}/SU(8)

which implied that
D=4

{\cal N}=8
supergravity is invariant under nonlinearly realized
{\hbox{\mybb E}}_{7,+7}
symmetry and under local
SU(8)
gauge symmetry transformations.

The natural question of whether hidden
{\hbox{\mybb E}}_{7,+7}
is present in 11D supergravity was posed and solved
by de Witt and Nicolai in
deWit:1985iy
;
deWit:1986mz
.
When supergravity is linearized over a flat spacetime,
only the
SU(8)
part of the hidden
{\hbox{\mybb E}}_{7,+7}
symmetry can be seen. Our work shows how such hidden
SU(8)
symmetry of type IIB supergravity – and also of its type IIA cousin,– appears from quantization of superparticle in its spinor moving frame formulation supplemented by auxiliary bridge variables.

These auxiliary bridge variables parametrize the space of possible complex structures which can be introduced in the phase space of type IIB superparticle in its spinor moving frame formulation. This space is isomorphic to

SU(8)/SO(8)
coset. We have shown how to incorporate these bridge variables
into the superparticle Lagrangian in the so–called analytical basis of type IIB Lorentz harmonic superspace in such a way that the number of physical degrees of freedom of the dynamical system is not changed. This is possible because the bridge variables are Stückelberg fields of the type IIB superparticle mechanics. This is in consonance with the fact that the introduction of Stückelberg fields is inevitable to manifest the
{\hbox{\mybb E}}_{7,+7}
symmetry of
11D
supergravity which is otherwise hidden
deWit:1985iy
;
deWit:1986mz
.

To observe the hidden
SU(8)
symmetry of the linearized type IIA supergravity we have performed similar quantization of type IIA
10D
superparticle. This resulted in actually the same chiral (analytic) on shell superfield as the one describing quantum state spectrum of type IIB superparticle. However, to obtain this result we needed to introduce a covariantly constant eight-vector
k^{i}
carrying the index of v- representation of
SO(8)
. This can be related to the spacelike
10
-vector
k_{\mu}
defining the T-duality transformation from type IIA to type IIB superparticle. Notice that the necessity of the compactness of T-duality direction is not seen in the linearized supergravities which appear in the quantum state spectrum of superparticles.

In the final part of the paper we have done first steps towards generalization of the on-shell superfield description of linearized supergravity to on-shell superamplitudes of type IIB supergravity and type IIA theory. We have discussed spinor moving frame derivation of simple superamplitudes of type IIB theory first calculated in
Boels:2012ie
, and the application of such type IIB formulas and their higher point generalizations to type IIA superamplitudes. The superamplitudes with less or equal to seven IIA supergrvaitons allow for the description within such a formalism. This restriction follows from the necessity to define T-duality vector
k_{\mu}

which is
the same
for all scattered particles and orthogonal to all their momenta.

We have also described some stage towards calculation of superamplitudes of type IIA theory involving, besides type IIA supergrvaity multiplets (supergravitons) also D
0
–brane(s) (Dirichlet particle(s)). To this end we have used the results of recent quantization of D
0
–brane in its spinor moving frame formulation
Bandos:2025pxv
. We pointed out a problem which hampers the straightforward generalization of the approach used to derive superamplitudes of
type II supergravitons to this case. This issue can be followed to the difference of the action of type IIA supersymmetry on the reduced superspaces with complex structures on which the on-shell superfields of massive and massless superparticles are defined.

Probably the ’component amplitude’ method of solving the supersymmetric Ward identities generalizing
D=4
approach of
Bianchi:2008pu
will show itself more productive in this case.
Another alternative is to elaborate a type IIA massless and massive generalizations of the constrained superfield approach to superamplitude proposed for
D=10
SYM and
D=11
SUGRA in
Bandos:2017eof
.

Appendix A
Some useful relations for 10D spinor moving frame variables

A.1
Inverse spinor moving frame matrix

The rectangular blocks
v^{-\alpha}_{\dot{q}}
and
v^{+\alpha}_{{q}}
of the inverse to the spinor moving frame matrix (
2.10
),

\displaystyle V_{(\beta)}^{\;\;\;\alpha}=\left(\begin{matrix}v^{-\alpha}_{\dot{q}}\cr v^{+\alpha}_{{q}}\end{matrix}\right)\in Spin(1,9)\;\qquad

(A.1)

obey
v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}v^{-\gamma}_{\dot{q}}+v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}v^{+\gamma}_{q}=\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\gamma}
and

\displaystyle v^{-\alpha}_{\dot{q}}v_{\alpha p}^{\;+}=\delta_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}\;,\qquad

\displaystyle v^{+\alpha}_{{q}}v_{\alpha\dot{p}}^{\;+}=0\;,\qquad

(A.2)

\displaystyle v^{-\alpha}_{\dot{q}}v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}=0\;,\qquad

\displaystyle v^{+\alpha}_{{q}}v_{\alpha{p}}^{\;-}=\delta_{qp}\;.\qquad

They are related with
moving frame vectors by the constraints similar to (
2.22
)–(
2.24
),

\displaystyle u_{a}^{=}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{a\,\alpha\beta}=2v^{-\alpha}_{\dot{q}}v^{-\beta}_{\dot{q}}\;,\qquad v^{-}_{\dot{q}}{\sigma}_{a}v^{-}_{\dot{p}}=u_{a}^{=}\delta_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}\;,\qquad

(A.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{{a}{\alpha}{\beta}}u_{{a}}^{\#}=2v_{{q}}^{+{\alpha}}v_{{q}}^{+}{}^{{\beta}}\;,\qquad v_{{q}}^{+}{\Gamma}_{{a}}v_{{p}}^{+}=\;u_{{a}}^{\#}\delta_{{q}{p}}\;,\qquad

(A.4)

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{{a}{\alpha}{\beta}}u_{{a}}^{i}=-2v_{\dot{q}}^{-({\alpha}}\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{q}}v_{{q}}^{+}{}^{{\beta})}\;,\qquad v_{\dot{q}}^{-}{\sigma}_{{a}}v_{{p}}^{+}=-u_{{a}}^{i}\gamma^{i}_{p\dot{q}}\;.\qquad

(A.5)

A.2
Cartan forms and admissible variations of spinor moving frame variables

As spinor moving frame variables
v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}
and
v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}
parametrize double covering
Spin(1,9)
of the Lorentz group
SO(1,9)
, (
2.10
), their admissible variations, i.e. variations which preserve the constraints (
2.10
), or equivalently, Eqs. (
2.22
)–(
2.24
), should parametrize the space co–target to the Lorentz group, which is dual to the
so(1,9)
Lie algebra
25
25

25

More precisely, this co-tangent space is dual to the tangent space to the Lorentz group which is the space of vector fields representing
so(1,9)
Lie algebra.
. The same applies to admissible derivatives of the spinor frame variables, this is to say derivatives which preserve constraints (
2.22
)–(
2.24
) or condition (
2.10
).

As the moving frame vectors are composites of the spinor frame variables according to (
2.22
)–(
2.24
), they both carry the same
local
degrees of freedom.
Then it is convenient to define Cartan forms providing the basis in the space co-tangent to
Spin(1,9)
group in terms of moving frame vectors,

\displaystyle\Omega^{=i}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle u_{a}^{=}du^{ai}\;,\qquad

(A.6)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\#i}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle u_{a}^{\#}du^{ai}\;,\qquad

(A.7)

\displaystyle\Omega^{(0)}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{1\over 4}u_{a}^{=}du^{a\#}\;,\qquad

(A.8)

\displaystyle\Omega^{ij}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle u_{a}^{i}du^{aj}\;.\qquad

(A.9)

Of these, 1-forms (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) are covariant under local
SO(1,1)\otimes SO(8)
transformations and form the basis of the (space co-tangent to the) coset
\frac{SO(1,9)}{SO(1,1)\otimes SO(8)}
, while (
A.8
) and (
A.9
) transform as
SO(1,1)
and
SO(8)
connection, respectively. Furthermore, Cartan form (
A.6
),

\Omega^{=I}:=u_{a}^{=}du^{aI}
can be considered as a basis of the coset
\frac{SO(1,9)}{[SO(1,1)\otimes SO(8)]\subset\!\!\!\!\times K_{8}}={\hbox{\mybb S}}^{8}
, isomorphic to celestial sphere of a 10D observer
Delduc:1991ir
;
Galperin:1991gk
.

Using the consequence

\displaystyle\delta_{a}{}^{b}=\frac{1}{2}u_{a}^{=}u^{b\#}+\frac{1}{2}u_{a}^{\#}u^{b=}-u_{a}^{i}u^{bi}\;\qquad

(A.10)

of Eqs. (
2.25
), (
2.26
),
the (admissible) derivatives of moving frame vectors can be easily expressed in terms of the above Cartan forms.
We prefer to write these relations as expressions for the
SO(1,1)\times SO(8)
covariant derivatives of moving frame vectors,

\displaystyle Du^{=}_{a}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle du^{=}_{a}+2u^{=}_{a}\Omega^{(0)}=u^{i}_{a}\Omega^{=i}\;,\qquad

(A.11)

\displaystyle Du^{\#}_{a}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle du^{\#}_{a}-2u^{\#}_{a}\Omega^{(0)}=u^{i}_{a}\Omega^{\#i}\;,\qquad

(A.12)

\displaystyle Du^{i}_{a}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle du^{i}_{a}+u^{j}_{a}\Omega^{ji}={1\over 2}u^{\#}_{a}\Omega^{=i}+{1\over 2}u^{=}_{a}\Omega^{\#i}\;.\qquad

(A.13)

The self consistency conditions of Eqs. (
A.11
), (
A.12
) and (
A.13
), this is to say Ricci identities

\displaystyle DDu^{=}_{a}=2u^{=}_{a}d\Omega^{(0)},\qquad DDu^{\#}_{a}=-2u^{\#}_{a}d\Omega^{(0)}\;,\qquad DDu^{i}_{a}=u^{j}_{a}G^{ji}=u^{j}_{a}(d\Omega^{ji}+\Omega^{jk}\wedge\Omega^{ki})\;,

(A.14)

are equivalent to the Maurer-Cartan equations of the
SO(1,9)
group

\displaystyle D\Omega^{=i}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle d\Omega^{=i}+2\Omega^{=i}\wedge\Omega^{(0)}+\Omega^{ij}\wedge\Omega^{=j}=0\;,\qquad

(A.15)

\displaystyle D\Omega^{\#i}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle d\Omega^{\#i}-2\Omega^{\#i}\wedge\Omega^{(0)}+\Omega^{ij}\wedge\Omega^{\#j}=0\;,\qquad

(A.16)

\displaystyle d\Omega^{(0)}={1\over 4}\Omega^{=i}\wedge\Omega^{\#i}\;,\qquad

(A.17)

\displaystyle G^{ij}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle d\Omega^{ij}+\Omega^{ik}\wedge\Omega^{kj}=-\Omega^{=[i}\wedge\Omega^{\#j]}\;.\qquad

(A.18)

As the co–tangent space to
Spin(1,D-1)
is isomorphic to
the space co–tangent to
SO(1,D-1)
, the derivatives of spinor moving frame variables are expressed in terms of the same Cartan forms. These expressions can be derived, after some algebra, by taking derivatives of the constraints (
2.22
)–(
2.24
) and using (
A.11
)–(
A.13
). In such a way we arrive at the following expression for derivatives (and covariant derivatives) of spinor moving frame variables

\displaystyle Dv_{\alpha{q}}^{\;-}:=dv_{\alpha{q}}^{\;-}+\Omega^{(0)}v_{\alpha{q}}^{\;-}+{1\over 4}\Omega^{ij}v_{\alpha{p}}^{\;-}\gamma_{{p}{q}}^{ij}={1\over 2}\Omega^{=i}\gamma_{q\dot{q}}^{i}v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}\;,\qquad

(A.19)

\displaystyle Dv_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}:=dv_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}-\Omega^{(0)}v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}+{1\over 4}\Omega^{ij}v_{\alpha\dot{p}}^{\;+}\tilde{\gamma}_{\dot{p}\dot{q}}^{ij}={1\over 2}v_{\alpha{q}}^{\;-}\Omega^{\#i}\gamma_{q\dot{q}}^{i}\;,\qquad

(A.20)

as well as of the blocks of inverse spinor moving frame matrix (which we will also call spinor moving frame variables below),

\displaystyle Dv_{\dot{q}}^{-\alpha}:=dv_{\dot{q}}^{-\alpha}+\Omega^{(0)}v_{\dot{q}}^{-\alpha}+{1\over 4}\Omega^{ij}\tilde{\gamma}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}^{ij}v_{\dot{p}}^{-\alpha}=-{1\over 2}\Omega^{=i}v_{{q}}^{+\alpha}\gamma_{q\dot{q}}^{i}\;,\qquad

(A.21)

\displaystyle Dv_{\dot{q}}^{+\alpha}:=dv_{\dot{q}}^{+\alpha}-\Omega^{(0)}v_{\dot{q}}^{+\alpha}+{1\over 4}\Omega^{ij}v_{\dot{p}}^{+\alpha}\gamma_{\dot{p}\dot{q}}^{ij}=-{1\over 2}\Omega^{\#i}v_{p}^{-\alpha}\gamma_{p\dot{q}}^{i}\;.\qquad

(A.22)

Here
\gamma^{i}_{p\dot{q}}=\tilde{\gamma}^{i}_{\dot{q}p}
are
SO(8)
Clebsch-Gordan coefficients which obey
(
2.16
), and
\gamma^{ij}=\gamma^{[i}\tilde{\gamma}^{j]}
,
\tilde{\gamma}^{ij}=\tilde{\gamma}^{[i}{\gamma}^{j]}
, so that

\displaystyle\gamma^{i}\tilde{\gamma}^{j}=\delta^{ij}{\hbox{\mybb I}}_{8\times 8}+\gamma^{ij}\;,\qquad\tilde{\gamma}^{i}{\gamma}^{j}=\delta^{ij}{\hbox{\mybb I}}_{8\times 8}+\tilde{\gamma}^{ij}\;.\qquad

(A.23)

The admissible variations of the spinor moving frame variables can be obtained formally by contraction of the
above expressions of exterior derivative with variational symbol using the rule

i_{\delta}d=\delta
(which is the Lie derivative formula
(-)\delta=i_{\delta}d+di_{\delta}
applied to 0-forms).
In this way we obtain, in particular,

\displaystyle\delta u^{=}_{a}=-2u^{=}_{a}i_{\delta}\Omega^{(0)}+u^{i}_{a}i_{\delta}\Omega^{=i}\;,\qquad

(A.24)

\displaystyle\delta v_{\alpha{q}}^{\;-}=-i_{\delta}\Omega^{(0)}v_{\alpha{q}}^{\;-}-{1\over 4}i_{\delta}\Omega^{ij}v_{\alpha{p}}^{\;-}\gamma_{{p}{q}}^{ij}+{1\over 2}i_{\delta}\Omega^{=i}\gamma_{q\dot{q}}^{i}v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}\;.\qquad\qquad

(A.25)

A.3
Covariant derivatives for Lorentz harmonic coordinates

The appropriate differential operators in the harmonic sector of Lorentz harmonic superspace are the so-called covariant harmonic derivatives (their counterparts in
SU(2)/U(1)

{\cal N}=2
and
SU(3)/[U(1)\times U(1)]

{\cal N}=3
harmonic superspaces were introduced in
Galperin:1984av
;
Galperin:1984bu
),

\displaystyle D^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{v}_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}}-v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}}\;,\qquad

(A.26)

\displaystyle D^{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{v}_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ij}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha\dot{p}}^{\;+}}+\frac{1}{2}v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}\gamma^{ij}_{qp}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha p}^{\;-}}\;,\qquad

(A.27)

\displaystyle D^{\#i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{v}_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{i}_{\dot{q}p}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha p}^{\;-}}\;,\qquad

(A.28)

\displaystyle D^{=i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{q\dot{p}}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha\dot{p}}^{\;+}}\;.\qquad

(A.29)

Their characteristic property is that they commute with all the defining constraints (
2.22
)–(
2.24
). Their form can be derived algorithmically by decomposing the exterior differential acting in harmonic sector of Lorentz harmonic superspace, this is to say on
Spin(1,9)
group, on the Cartan forms (
A.6
)–(
A.9
)

\displaystyle d^{Spin(1,9)}:=dv_{\alpha q}^{\;-}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha q}^{\;-}}+d{v}_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}}=\Omega^{(0)}D^{(0)}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{ij}D^{ij}+\Omega^{=i}D^{\#i}+\Omega^{\#i}D^{=i}\;.\qquad

(A.30)

The r.h.s. is obtained from the l.h.s. by expressing
dv_{\alpha q}^{\;-}
and
d{v}_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+}
through Cartan forms using Eqs.
(
A.19
) and (
A.20
).

This fact also manifests the duality of
the basis of cotangent space to
(the double covering
Spin(1,9)
of) the Lorentz group SO(1,9), given by Cartan forms (
A.6
)–(
A.9
), to the basis of the space tangent to (the double covering
Spin(1,9)
of) SO(1,9) provided by the covariant harmonic derivatives (
A.26
)–(
A.29
).

The commutators of these covariant derivatives represent the algebra
so(1,9)
,

\displaystyle{}[D^{(0)},D^{ij}]=0\;,\qquad{}[D^{(0)},D^{\#i}]=2D^{\#i}\;,\qquad{}[D^{(0)},D^{=i}]=-2D^{=i}\;,\qquad

(A.31)

\displaystyle{}[D^{ij},D^{kl}]=4\delta^{[i|[k}D^{l]|j]}\;,\qquad{}[D^{ij},D^{\#k}]=2D^{\#[i}\delta^{j]k}\;,\qquad{}[D^{ij},D^{=k}]=2D^{=[i}\delta^{j]k}\;,\qquad

(A.32)

\displaystyle{}[D^{\#i},D^{\#j}]=0\;,\qquad{}[D^{\#i},D^{=j}]=\frac{1}{4}\delta^{ij}D^{(0)}+\frac{1}{2}D^{ij}\;,\qquad{}[D^{=i},D^{=j}]=0\;,\qquad

(A.33)

which is natural as the target space of the double covering
Spin(1,9)
of the Lorentz group
SO(1,9)
is isomorphic to the Lorentz algebra
so(1,9)
.

The action of these covariant derivatives on the vector harmonics can of course be calculated taking into account its composite nature reflected by the constraints (
2.22
)–(
2.24
). However it is much more practical to obtain this by decomposing the differential in the group manifold of
SO(1,9)
parametrized by vector harmonics

\displaystyle d^{SO(1,9)}:=du_{a}^{=}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{=}}+du_{a}^{\#}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{\#}}+du_{a}^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{i}}=\Omega^{(0)}D^{(0)}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{ij}D^{ij}+\Omega^{=i}D^{\#i}+\Omega^{\#i}D^{=i}\;.\qquad

(A.34)

On this way
we find the following expressions for covariant harmonic derivatives

\displaystyle D^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2u_{a}^{\#}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{\#}}-2u_{a}^{=}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{=}}\;,\qquad

(A.35)

\displaystyle D^{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{a}^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{j}}-u_{a}^{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{i}}\;,\qquad

(A.36)

\displaystyle D^{\#i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}u_{a}^{\#}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{i}}+u_{a}^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{=}}\;,\qquad

(A.37)

\displaystyle D^{=i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}u_{a}^{=}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{i}}+u_{a}^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{a}^{\#}}\;\qquad

(A.38)

which can be used in the cases where only vector Lorentz harmonics (
2.18
), but not spinorial ones (
2.10
), are present.

Similarly one can find how covariant harmonic derivatives act on the element of the inverse harmonic variables - counterparts of the blocks of inverse spinor moving frame matrix (
A.1
).

The superfield of Lorentz harmonic superspace including coset
SO(1,9)/[SO(1,1)\otimes SO(8)]
are obtained by restricting the class of superfields defined on superspace with
SO(1,9)
group manifold by conditions of carrying definite representations of
SO(1,1)
and
SO(8)
group. This can be formulated by defining the result of the action of the differential operators

D^{(0)}
and
D^{ij}
on this class of superfields. Below we will find such type conditions as the result of quantization of massless superparticle mechanics.

A.4
Curvature and variations of the composite
SU(8)
connection

The curvature of the composite
SU(8)
connection (
2.93
) is expressed in terms of
SO(1,9)/[SO(1,1)\otimes SO(8)]
Cartan forms (
A.6
), (
A.7
) as

\displaystyle d{\mho}_{B}{}^{A}-{\mho}_{B}{}^{C}\wedge{\mho}_{C}{}^{A}=\frac{1}{4}\Omega^{=i}\wedge\Omega^{\#j}\gamma^{ij}{}_{B}{}^{A}\;,\qquad

(A.39)

where

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\gamma^{ij}{}_{B}{}^{A}=\frac{1}{4}\bar{{\rm w}}_{qB}\gamma^{ij}_{qp}{\rm w}_{p}{}^{A}\;.

(A.40)

This matrix, besides not
being constant, provides a representation of the
so(9)
generator.

Eq. (
A.39
) can be used to obtain the expression for admissible variations of the generalized
SU(8)
Cartan forms,

\displaystyle\delta{\mho}_{B}{}^{A}={\mho}_{B}{}^{C}\,i_{\delta}{\mho}_{C}{}^{A}-i_{\delta}{\mho}_{B}{}^{C}\,{\mho}_{C}{}^{A}+(\Omega^{=i}\,i_{\delta}\Omega^{\#j}-i_{\delta}\Omega^{=i}\,\Omega^{\#j})\frac{1}{4}\gamma^{ij}{}_{B}{}^{A}\;.

(A.41)

In it
i_{\delta}{\mho}_{B}{}^{A}
parametrize local
SU(8)
transformation which is the manifest gauge symmetry of the Lagrangian 1–form (
2.100
). Notice that
{\mho}_{B}{}^{A}
is invariant under
SO(1,1)\otimes SO(8)
symmetry acting on the spinor frame variables, but its variations transformed by
{\hbox{\mybb K}}_{8}
symmetry (
i_{\delta}\Omega^{\#j}
) and under the most essential variations of spinor frame variables (
i_{\delta}\Omega^{=i}
) are nontrivial.

Appendix B
SO(8) gamma matrices

The exhaustive list of the useful relations for
SO(8)
gamma matrices

\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}=:\tilde{\gamma}{}^{i}_{q\dot{p}}\;,\qquad i=1,...,8\;,\qquad q,p=1,...,8\;,\qquad\dot{q},\dot{p}=1,...,8\;

(B.1)

can be found in Appendix A of
Green:1983hw
. Here we reproduce some of these relations for readers convenience.

First of all, they obey

\displaystyle\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}\gamma^{j}_{p\dot{p}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\gamma^{i}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{j})_{q{p}}=\delta^{ij}\delta_{q{p}}+\gamma^{ij}_{q{p}}\;,\qquad

(B.2)

\displaystyle\gamma^{i}_{p\dot{q}}\gamma^{j}_{p\dot{p}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\tilde{\gamma}{}^{i}{\gamma}{}^{j})_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}=\delta^{ij}\delta_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}+\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ij}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}\;,\qquad

(B.3)

\displaystyle\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{q}}\gamma^{i}_{p\dot{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{q{p}}\delta_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}+\frac{1}{4}\gamma^{ij}_{q{p}}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ij}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}=:\delta_{q{p}}\delta_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}+\gamma^{qp}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}\;,

(B.4)

where, following
Green:1983hw
, we introduced
\gamma^{qp}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}=\frac{1}{4}\gamma^{ij}_{q{p}}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ij}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}
to make manifest that these relations respect the well known triality property of
SO(8)
group.
The triality properties implies equivalence of the vector
8
v
s-spinor
8
s
and c-spinor
8
c
representations of

SO(8)
and imply that in any relation one can interchange
i,q
and
\dot{q}
indices.

The Fierz identity for the matrix with two s-spinor indices

M_{qp}=\frac{1}{8}\delta_{qp}{\rm tr}(M)-\frac{1}{16}\gamma^{ij}_{q{p}}{\rm tr}(\gamma^{ij}M)+\frac{1}{2\cdot 8\cdot 4!}\gamma^{ijkl}_{q{p}}{\rm tr}(\gamma^{ijkl}M)\;\qquad

(B.5)

uses

\delta_{q[r}\delta_{s]p}=-\frac{1}{16}\gamma^{ij}_{qp}\gamma^{ij}_{rs}\;,\qquad

(B.6)

\delta_{q(r}\delta_{s)p}=\frac{1}{8}\delta_{qp}\delta_{rs}+\frac{1}{384}\gamma^{ijkl}_{qp}\gamma^{ijkl}_{rs}\;,\qquad

(B.7)

and/or

\text{tr}(\gamma^{ij}\gamma^{kl})=-16\delta_{[i}{}^{k}\delta_{j]}{}^{l}\;,\qquad\text{tr}(\gamma^{ijkl}\gamma^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}})=8\,(4!\,\delta_{[i}{}^{i^{\prime}}\ldots\delta_{l]}{}^{l^{\prime}}+\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}})\;.\qquad

(B.8)

Notice that the last of this equation reflects self-duality of
\gamma^{ijkl}
,

\gamma^{ijkl}=\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\gamma^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ijkl}=-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\qquad

(B.9)

which then implies

\displaystyle\gamma^{ijkli^{\prime}}=-\frac{1}{3!}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\gamma^{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\qquad

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ijkli^{\prime}}=+\frac{1}{3!}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\qquad

(B.10)

\displaystyle\gamma^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\gamma^{k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\qquad

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}}=+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\;,\qquad

(B.11)

\displaystyle\gamma^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}=+\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\gamma^{l^{\prime}}\qquad

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}=-\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{l^{\prime}}\;,\qquad

(B.12)

\displaystyle\gamma^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}=\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}{\hbox{\mybb I}}\qquad

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}=-\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}{\hbox{\mybb I}}\;,\qquad

(B.13)

To proceed, we introduce

\displaystyle t^{i}_{qpr\dot{s}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\gamma^{ij}_{[qp}\gamma^{j}_{r]\dot{s}}\;,\qquad

(B.14)

\displaystyle t^{ij}_{qprs}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\gamma^{ik}_{[qp}\gamma^{jk}_{rs]}=t^{ji}_{qprs}\;,\qquad

(B.15)

\displaystyle t^{ijkl}_{qprs}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\gamma^{[ij}_{[qp}\gamma^{kl]}_{rs]}\;,\qquad

(B.16)

which obey

\displaystyle t^{ij}_{qprs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{qprsq^{\prime}p^{\prime}r^{\prime}s^{\prime}}t^{ij}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}r^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\;,\qquad

(B.17)

\displaystyle t^{ijkl}_{qprs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{qprsq^{\prime}p^{\prime}r^{\prime}s^{\prime}}t^{ijkl}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}r^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\;,\qquad

(B.18)

\displaystyle t^{ijkl}_{qprs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}t^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}r^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\;.\qquad

(B.19)

Furthermore (
B.8
) implies that
t^{ij}_{qprs}=t^{ji}_{qprs}
is traceless on its v-indices

t^{ii}_{qprs}=0\;.

(B.20)

The signs in (
B.17
)–(
B.19
) correspond to the sign plus in

\displaystyle\gamma_{qp}^{ijkl}=\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\gamma_{qp}^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\;,\qquad

(B.21)

which completes the description of the conventions for gamma matrices.

Notice that (
B.17
) and (
B.18
) imply that

t^{ijkl}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}t^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}=0\;.\qquad

(B.22)

As far as the other traces over four antisymmetrized blocks of SO(8) s-spinor indices are concerned, it is not difficult to find that

\displaystyle t^{ij}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}t^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}=384\,\left(\delta^{i(i^{\prime}}\delta^{j^{\prime})j}-\frac{1}{8}\delta^{ij}\delta^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\right)\;,\qquad

(B.23)

and

\displaystyle t^{ijkl}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}t^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}=128\;\left(\delta_{i^{\prime}}^{[i}\delta_{j^{\prime}}^{j}\delta_{k^{\prime}}^{k}\delta_{l^{\prime}}^{l]}-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ijkli^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\right)\;.\qquad

(B.24)

Some other very important equations are

\displaystyle\gamma^{ij}_{[qp}\gamma^{kl}_{rs]}=t^{ijkl}_{qprs}-\frac{2}{3}\delta^{[i|[k}t^{l]|j]}_{qprs}\;,\qquad

(B.25)

\displaystyle t^{i}_{q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}\dot{q}}t^{j}_{q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}\dot{p}}=48\delta^{ij}\delta_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}-6(\tilde{\gamma}{}^{i}\gamma^{j})_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}\;.\qquad

(B.26)

It is easy to check that these are consistent with (
B.15
), (
B.16
) and the following consequence of (
B.6
):

t^{i}_{q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}\dot{q}}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{i}_{\dot{q}p}=0\;.

(B.27)

More useful identities are

\displaystyle t^{ijkl}_{qp_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}t^{i^{\prime}}_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\dot{q}}=\frac{1}{3}(\gamma^{[ij|}\gamma^{j^{\prime}})_{q\dot{q}}\;tr(\gamma^{|kl]}\gamma^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}})-\frac{2}{3}(\gamma^{[ij|}\gamma^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\gamma^{|kl]}\gamma^{j^{\prime}})_{q\dot{q}}=

\displaystyle=-\frac{16}{3}(\gamma^{[ijk})_{q\dot{q}}\delta^{l]i^{\prime}}+\frac{32}{3}\delta^{i^{\prime}[i}\gamma^{jkl]}_{q\dot{q}}-2\gamma^{ijkl}\gamma^{i^{\prime}}\gamma^{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}_{q\dot{q}}=

\displaystyle=-8\left(\delta^{i^{\prime}[i}\gamma^{jkl]}-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{i^{\prime}ijklj^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\gamma^{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\right)_{q\dot{q}}

(B.28)

(which is in consonance with anti-self duality of
t^{ijkl}_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}p_{4}}
(
B.19
)) and

\displaystyle t^{ij}_{qp_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}t^{i^{\prime}}_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\dot{q}}=\frac{1}{3}(\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{j^{\prime}})_{q\dot{q}}\;tr(\gamma^{jk}\gamma^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}})-\frac{2}{3}(\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\gamma^{jk}\gamma^{j^{\prime}})_{q\dot{q}}=

\displaystyle=-48\delta^{i^{\prime}(i}\gamma^{j)}_{q\dot{q}}+6\delta^{ij}\gamma^{i^{\prime}}_{q\dot{q}}

(B.29)

(which is in consonance with symmetricity and tracelessness of
t^{ij}_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}p_{4}}
(
B.20
)).

Appendix C
SU(8) and SO(7)

As in
Bandos:2025pxv
, we will use the set of 7 complex
8\times 8
matrices obtained by
SU(8)
rotation (on
Spin(7)
indices) of the
SO(7)
Clebsch-Gordan coefficients (generalized Pauli matrices),

\sigma^{\check{I}}_{AB}=-\sigma^{\check{I}}_{BA}=\sigma^{\check{I}}_{[AB]}\;

(C.1)

and their complex conjugate

\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{I}\;AB}=-\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{I}\;BA}\dot{=}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{I}\;[AB]}=-(\sigma^{\check{I}}_{AB})^{*}=(\sigma^{\check{I}}_{BA})^{*}\;

(C.2)

which obey

\sigma^{\check{I}}_{AC}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{J}\;CB}+\sigma^{\check{J}}_{AC}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{I}\;CB}:=2\sigma^{(\check{I}}_{AC}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{J})\;CB}=\delta_{A}{}^{B}\;.

(C.3)

The complex symmetric matrix

{\cal U}_{AB}=\frac{1}{7!}\epsilon^{\check{I}_{1}\ldots\check{I}_{7}}\sigma^{\check{I}_{1}\ldots\check{I}_{7}}_{AB}\;\qquad

(C.4)

is unitary,

{\cal U}_{AC}\bar{{\cal U}}{}^{CB}=\delta_{A}{}^{B}\;\qquad

(C.5)

and parametrizes the coset
SU(8)/SO(7)
. It is not constant but is obtained from 8
\times 8
unity matrix (invariant tensor of
SO(8)
) by
SU(8)
transformations (and thus represents these transformations modulo their
SO(8)
part).
Keeping this in mind the matrix
{\cal U}_{AB}
and its c.c.

\bar{{\cal U}}{}^{AB}
can be used to rise and to lower the
SU(8)
indices, in particular

({\cal U}\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{J}})_{A}{}^{B}=-(\sigma^{\check{J}}\bar{{\cal U}})_{A}{}^{B}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{J}AB}=-(\bar{{\cal U}}{\sigma}^{\check{J}}\bar{{\cal U}})^{AB}\;.\qquad

(C.6)

See Appendix A of
Bandos:2025pxv
for more discussion and further details.

Finally, let us write down
an explicit representation for the basic matrices using SO(8) matrices
\gamma^{i}_{p\dot{q}}=\tilde{\gamma}^{i}_{\dot{q}p}=(\gamma^{\check{I}}_{p\dot{q}},\gamma^{8}_{p\dot{q}})
, obeying (
2.16
), and
(
2.91
) as SU(8) element:

\sigma^{\check{J}}_{AB}=(\gamma^{\check{J}}\tilde{\gamma}^{8})_{pq}\bar{w}_{pA}\bar{w}_{qB}\;,\qquad\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\check{J}AB}=({\gamma}^{8}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{\check{J}})_{pq}w_{p}^{A}w_{q}^{B}\;.

(C.7)

Also notice that the identity for
SO(8)
matrices give rise to the following identities

\displaystyle\sigma^{\check{J}}_{AB}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{CD}+{\cal U}_{AB}{\cal U}_{CD}={\cal U}_{AC}{\cal U}_{BD}-\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{AC}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{BD}+\frac{1}{4}(\sigma^{\check{J}\check{K}}{\cal U})_{AC}(\sigma^{\check{J}\check{K}}{\cal U})_{BD}

(C.8)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\qquad\sigma^{\check{J}}_{A[B}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{CD]}=-\frac{1}{2}(\sigma^{\check{J}\check{K}}{\cal U})_{A[B}(\sigma^{\check{J}\check{K}}{\cal U})_{CD]}\;\qquad

(C.9)

as well as to the anti–duality relation between complex conjugate matrices

\displaystyle t^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}\check{L}}_{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{[\check{I}\check{J}}{\cal U})_{[AB}(\sigma^{\check{K}\check{L}]}{\cal U})_{CD]}\;,\qquad

(C.10)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}\check{L}}^{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\tilde{\sigma}^{[\check{I}\check{J}|}\bar{{\cal U}})^{[AB}(\tilde{\sigma}^{|\check{K}\check{L}]}\bar{{\cal U}})^{CD]}=(t^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}\check{L}}_{ABCD})^{*}\;,\qquad

(C.11)

and duality between

\displaystyle t^{\check{I}\check{J}}_{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\check{I}\check{K}}{\cal U})_{[AB}(\sigma^{\check{J}\check{K}}{\cal U})_{CD]}+\sigma^{\check{I}}_{[AB}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{CD]}\;,\qquad

(C.12)

\displaystyle t^{\check{I}}_{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\sigma^{[\check{I}\check{K}}{\cal U})_{[AB}\sigma^{\check{K}]}{}_{CD]}\;,\qquad

(C.13)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}\check{J}}^{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{I}\check{K}}\bar{{\cal U}})^{[AB}(\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{J}\check{K}]}\bar{{\cal U}})^{CD]}+\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{I}[AB}\tilde{\sigma}^{CD]\check{J}}=(t^{\check{I}\check{J}}_{ABCD})^{*}\;,\qquad

(C.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}}^{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{I}\check{K}}\bar{{\cal U}})^{[AB}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{CD]\,\check{K}}=(t^{\check{I}}_{ABCD})^{*}\;,

(C.15)

i.e.

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}\check{L}}^{ABCD}=-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ABCDEFGH}t^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}\check{L}}_{EFGH}\;,\qquad

(C.16)

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}\check{J}}^{ABCD}=+\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ABCDEFGH}t^{\check{I}\check{J}}_{EFGH}\;,\qquad

(C.17)

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}}^{ABCD}=+\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ABCDEFGH}t^{\check{I}}_{EFGH}\;.\qquad

(C.18)

Actually, instead of (
C.10
) and (
C.11
) it is more convenient to use their anti-duals
with three
SO(7)
vector indices

\displaystyle t^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}}_{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{[\check{I}\check{J}}{\cal U})_{[AB}\sigma^{\check{K}]}{}_{CD]}=-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}\check{L}\check{P}\check{Q}\check{R}}\tilde{t}^{\check{L}\check{P}\check{Q}\check{R}}_{EFGH}\;,\qquad

(C.19)

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}}^{ABCD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\tilde{\sigma}^{[\check{I}\check{J}|}\bar{{\cal U}})^{[AB}\tilde{\sigma}^{CD]\,|\check{K}]}=(t^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}}_{ABCD})^{*}=-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon_{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}\check{L}\check{P}\check{Q}\check{R}}\tilde{t}_{\check{L}\check{P}\check{Q}\check{R}}^{ABCD}\;,\qquad

(C.20)

which are also anti-dual

\displaystyle\tilde{t}{}_{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}}^{ABCD}=-\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ABCDEFGH}t^{\check{I}\check{J}\check{K}}_{EFGH}\;.\qquad

(C.21)

With our representation (
C.7
), the identities for
SO(8)
gamma (actually sigma) matrices (first presented, to our best knowledge in
Green:1982tk
, and collected in Appendix
B
) also imply the duality relation for contraction of sigma matrices on their 7-vector indices

\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{I}[AB}\tilde{\sigma}^{CD]\check{I}}=+\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon^{ABCDEFGH}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{EF}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{GH}\;.

(C.22)

We did not include these matrices in the above list of basic elements for decomposition of antisymmetric tensors
because it appears as a trace of symmetric
SO(7)
tensor (
C.12
),

\displaystyle t^{\check{J}\check{J}}_{ABCD}=-\sigma^{\check{J}}_{[AB}\sigma^{\check{J}}_{CD]}\;,\qquad\tilde{t}_{\check{J}\check{J}}^{ABCD}=-\tilde{\sigma}^{\check{J}[AB}\tilde{\sigma}^{CD]\check{J}}\;.\qquad

(C.23)

Appendix D
Some technical details for on spinor moving frame
derivation of
3
-point type IIB superamplitude and on related issues relevant to discussion of type IIA

D.1
Some properties of
\hat{K}_{q[\dot{q}|}
as well as of
{\rm s}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}
(
5.47
) and its cousins

Notice the useful relations involving the unitary matrix
(
5.39
)

\displaystyle\hat{K}_{q_{1}[\dot{q}_{1}|}\ldots\hat{K}_{q_{4}|\dot{q}_{4}]}=2\,4!\,{\rm s}_{q_{1}\ldots q_{4}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}

(D.24)

and

\displaystyle\hat{K}_{q[\dot{q}|}\hat{K}_{p|\dot{p}}:=4{\rm s}_{q}{}^{\tilde{A}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}_{[\dot{q}|\tilde{A}}{\rm s}_{p}{}^{\tilde{B}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}_{|\dot{p}]\tilde{B}}=\frac{1}{16}\hat{K}_{q}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ij}\tilde{\hat{K}}_{p}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{kl}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}=\frac{1}{16}\tilde{\hat{K}}_{\dot{q}}{\gamma}{}^{ij}\hat{K}_{\dot{p}}{\gamma}{}^{ij}_{qp}=\qquad

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{256}{\rm tr}\left(\tilde{\hat{K}}{\gamma}{}^{ij}\hat{K}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{kl}\right){\gamma}{}^{ij}_{qp}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{kl}_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}=\frac{1}{256}{\rm tr}\left(\tilde{\hat{K}}{\gamma}{}^{ij}\hat{K}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{ij}\right){\gamma}{}^{ij}_{qp}{\gamma}{}^{kl}_{rs}{\gamma}{}^{i^{\prime}}_{r\dot{q}}{\gamma}{}^{i^{\prime}}_{s\dot{p}}\;.\qquad

(D.25)

These equations can be considered as an evidence (but not the proof) of the existence of relations

\displaystyle-\hat{K}^{i}K^{j}t^{ij}_{qprs}=\hat{K}_{[q|\dot{q}}\hat{K}_{|p|\dot{p}}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{|r|\dot{q}}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{|s]\dot{p}}=4s_{[q}^{\tilde{A}}s_{p}^{\tilde{B}}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{r|\dot{q}}\bar{\tilde{s}}_{\dot{q}\tilde{A}}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{|s]\dot{p}}\bar{\tilde{s}}_{\dot{p}\tilde{B}}=\qquad

\displaystyle=8\epsilon_{\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\tilde{C}\tilde{D}}{\rm s}_{q}^{\tilde{A}}{\rm s}_{p}^{\tilde{B}}{\rm s}_{r}^{\tilde{C}}{\rm s}_{s}^{\tilde{D}}=8\,4!\,{\rm s}_{qprs}\;.

(D.26)

Such equation was presented (in a bit different notation) and essentially used in
Boels:2012ie

(see Eqs. (4.27)–(4.30) of this paper). We have used it for relating amplitudes and superamplitudes in our approach.
Eqs. (
D.1
) can be obtained from

{\gamma}{}^{i}_{[q|\dot{q}}\bar{\tilde{s}}_{\dot{q}\tilde{A}}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{|p]\dot{p}}\bar{\tilde{s}}_{\dot{p}\tilde{B}}=2\epsilon_{\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\tilde{C}\tilde{D}}{\rm s}_{q}^{\tilde{C}}{\rm s}_{p}^{\tilde{D}}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad{\rm s}_{q}^{\tilde{A}}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{q[\dot{q}|}{\rm s}_{q}^{\tilde{B}}{\gamma}{}^{i}_{p|\dot{p}]}=2\epsilon^{\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\tilde{C}\tilde{D}}\bar{\tilde{s}}_{\dot{q}\tilde{C}}\bar{\tilde{s}}_{\dot{p}\tilde{D}}\;.\qquad

(D.27)

However, the consistency of imposing such conditions on our

\bar{\tilde{\rm s}}
variables has to be proven.

The 4-th rank covariant tensors with indices of fundamental and anti-fundamental representations of SU(8) associated to
(
5.46
) and (
5.47
)
are

\displaystyle\tilde{{\rm s}}{}^{A_{1}\ldots A_{4}}=\tilde{{\rm s}}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}w^{A_{1}}_{\dot{q}_{1}}\ldots w^{A_{4}}_{\dot{q}_{4}}=\,\frac{1}{4!}\,\epsilon_{\tilde{B}_{1}\ldots\tilde{B}_{4}}\tilde{{\rm s}}_{A_{1}}{}^{\tilde{B}_{1}}\ldots\tilde{{\rm s}}_{A_{4}}{}^{\tilde{B}_{4}}=(\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{4}})^{*}\;,\qquad

(D.28)

\displaystyle{{\rm s}}^{A_{1}\ldots A_{4}}={{\rm s}}_{{q}_{1}\ldots{q}_{4}}w^{A_{1}}_{\dot{q}_{1}}\ldots w^{A_{4}}_{\dot{q}_{4}}=\,\frac{1}{4!}\,\epsilon_{\tilde{B}_{1}\ldots\tilde{B}_{4}}{{\rm s}}_{A_{1}}{}^{\tilde{B}_{1}}\ldots{{\rm s}}_{A_{4}}{}^{\tilde{B}_{4}}=(\bar{{{\rm s}}}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{4}})^{*}\;.\qquad

(D.29)

D.2
On derivation of some amplitudes and of superamplitudes

Some details of the derivation of the amplitude (
5.53
) are

\displaystyle A(\bar{\phi}c^{i}\phi)=p_{{\rm 1}\mu}u^{\mu i}_{{\rm 2}}=\frac{1}{8}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}v_{\dot{q}{\rm 1}}^{-\alpha}v_{\dot{q}{\rm 1}}^{-\beta}\,{\sigma}{}^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}u{{}_{\mu}2}^{i}=\frac{1}{4}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}v_{\dot{q}{\rm 1}}^{-\alpha}v_{\dot{q}{\rm 1}}^{-\beta}\,v_{(\alpha|{q}{\rm 2}}^{\;-}\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}v_{|\beta)\dot{p}{\rm 2}}^{\;+}\,=\frac{1}{4}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}<\,{\rm 2}^{-}_{q}\,|\,{\rm 1}^{-}_{\dot{p}}\,>\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}=\qquad

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}\,|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|\,\beta_{{\rm 21}}{\rm s}_{q}{}^{\tilde{A}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}_{\dot{p}\tilde{A}}\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}=-\frac{1}{4}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}\,|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|\,\beta_{{\rm 21}}{\rm s}{}^{A\tilde{C}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}^{B}{}_{\tilde{C}}\gamma^{i}_{AB}\;.\qquad

(D.30)

To obtain this result, the Eqs. (
2.28
), (
2.24
), (
5.24
), (
5.44
), (
5.45
) and (
A.2
) have been used.

To make our expression closer to the one written
(for
D=8
) in
Boels:2012ie
we should consider

A(\bar{\phi}c_{q\dot{p}}\phi)=A(\bar{\phi}c^{i}\phi)\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}
. The calculations within spinor moving frame approach on the line described above give

\displaystyle A(\bar{\phi}c^{i}\phi)\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{{\rm 1}\mu}u^{\mu i}_{{\rm 2}}=-p_{{\rm 1}\mu}{\sigma}{}^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}v_{\dot{p}{\rm 2}}^{-\alpha}v_{{q}{\rm 2}}^{+\beta}=-2\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}<\,{\rm 1}^{-}_{q}\,|\,{\rm 2}^{-}_{\dot{p}}\,>=-2\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}\,|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|\,\beta_{{\rm 21}}{\rm s}_{q}{}^{\tilde{A}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}_{\dot{p}\tilde{A}}\;,\qquad

(D.31)

\displaystyle A(\bar{\phi}c^{i}\phi)\gamma^{i}_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}\,|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|\,\beta_{{\rm 21}}{\rm s}{}_{A}{}^{\tilde{C}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{B\tilde{C}}\;.

(D.32)

It is easy to check that, tracing these equations with

\gamma^{i}_{q\dot{p}}
and
\tilde{\bar{\gamma}}^{AB}
, respectively, we obtain (
D.2
).
Eq. (
D.31
) is close, although not identical to the one in
Boels:2012ie
.

The details on derivation of the final form of Eq.
(
5.55
) are

\displaystyle A(\bar{\phi}h^{ij}\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{{\rm 1}\mu}u^{\mu i}_{{\rm 2}}p_{{\rm 3}\nu}u^{\nu j}_{{\rm 2}}=\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 3}}u^{=}_{{\rm 1}\mu}u^{\mu i}_{{\rm 2}}u^{=}_{{\rm 3}\nu}u^{\nu j}_{{\rm 2}}

(D.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}}\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 3}}K^{=i}_{{\rm 21}}K^{=j}_{{\rm 23}}=-(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{2}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 3}})^{2}\;K^{=i}_{{\rm 31}}K^{=j}_{{\rm 31}}/(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{2}=-(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{2}K^{=i}_{{\rm 21}}K^{=j}_{{\rm 21}}\;.

One can immediately check that the
r.h.s.
of this expression is traceless on its
SO(8)
v-indices, as the
l.h.s.
should be.

D.3
Details of derivation of (
V.3
)

\displaystyle 2^{-24}\left((D^{+}_{{\rm 1}})^{\wedge 8}D^{+}_{{\rm 2}A}D^{+}_{{\rm 2}B}D^{+}_{{\rm 2}C}D^{+}_{{\rm 2}D}{\hbox{\mybb Q}}^{12}\right)|_{\Theta^{-}_{{\rm i}}=0}=\frac{1}{8!}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}\tilde{s}{}^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{12}}\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{1}A{\rm 2}}\ldots\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{4}D{\rm 2}}\epsilon^{B_{1}\ldots B_{8}}\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{5}B_{1}{\rm 1}}\ldots\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{12}B_{8}{\rm 1}}\qquad

(D.34)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8!}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}\tilde{s}{}^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{12}}\bar{w}_{q_{1}A}\ldots\bar{w}_{q_{4}D}{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{1}q_{1}{\rm 2}}\ldots{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{4}q_{4}{\rm 2}}\epsilon_{p_{1}\ldots p_{8}}{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{5}p_{1}{\rm 1}}\ldots{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{12}p_{8}{\rm 1}}\qquad

(D.35)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\bar{w}_{q_{1}A}\ldots\bar{w}_{q_{4}D}\tilde{{\rm s}}{}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}{v}{}^{-\alpha_{1}}_{\dot{p}_{1}{\rm 1}}\ldots{v}{}^{-\alpha_{4}}_{\dot{p}_{4}{\rm 1}}{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{1}q_{1}{\rm 2}}\ldots{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{4}q_{4}{\rm 2}}\qquad

(D.36)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\tilde{{\rm s}}{}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}\bar{w}_{q_{1}A}<\,2^{-}_{q_{1}}\,|\,1^{-}_{\dot{p}_{1}}>\,\ldots\bar{w}_{q_{4}D}\,<\,2^{-}_{q_{4}}\,|\,1^{-}_{\dot{p}_{4}}>\qquad

(D.37)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{16(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\tilde{{\rm s}}{}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}\bar{w}_{q_{1}A}K^{=k_{1}}_{{\rm 21}}\gamma^{k_{1}}_{q_{1}\dot{p}_{1}}\,\ldots\bar{w}_{q_{4}D}\,K^{=k_{4}}_{{\rm 21}}\gamma^{k_{4}}_{q_{4}\dot{p}_{4}}\,\qquad

(D.38)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\;|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|^{4}\;\tilde{{\rm s}}{}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}\bar{w}_{q_{1}A}{{\rm s}}{}_{q_{1}}{}^{\tilde{A_{1}}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{p}_{1}\tilde{A_{1}}}\,\ldots\bar{w}_{q_{4}D}\,{{\rm s}}{}_{q_{4}}{}^{\tilde{A_{4}}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{p}_{4}\tilde{A_{4}}}\,\qquad

(D.39)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\;|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 12}}|^{4}\;\tilde{{\rm s}}{}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}{\rm s}_{A}{}^{\tilde{A_{1}}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{p}_{1}\tilde{A_{1}}}\,\ldots\,{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{q_{4}}{}^{\tilde{A_{4}}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{p}_{4}\tilde{A_{4}}}\,\qquad

(D.40)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\;|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|^{4}\;\epsilon_{\tilde{A_{1}}\ldots\tilde{A_{4}}}\bar{s}_{A}{}^{\tilde{A_{1}}}\ldots\bar{s}_{D}{}^{\tilde{A_{4}}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}\,\qquad

(D.41)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\;|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|^{4}\;\epsilon_{\tilde{A_{1}}\ldots\tilde{A_{4}}}{\rm s}_{A}{}^{\tilde{A_{1}}}\ldots{\rm s}_{D}{}^{\tilde{A_{4}}}=4!\,\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\;|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|^{4}\;{\rm s}_{{}_{ABCD}}\,\qquad

(D.42)

\displaystyle=4!\,\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{8}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{4}}{(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\;|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|^{4}\;\bar{w}_{q_{1}A}\ldots\bar{w}_{q_{4}D}{\rm s}_{q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}\;\qquad

(D.43)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{2}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 2}})^{2}}{8\,(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\,|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}}|^{2}\;A(\bar{\phi}^{[+8]}\phi_{{}_{ABCD}}^{[+4]}\phi)=\frac{(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 1}})^{2}(\rho^{\#}_{{\rm 3}})^{2}}{8\,(\rho^{\#})^{2}}\,|\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 31}}|^{2}\;A(\bar{\phi}^{[+8]}\phi_{{}_{ABCD}}^{[+4]}\phi)\;.\qquad

(D.44)

To obtain the third line
of this series of equations, (
D.36
), we have used
the identity

\displaystyle\tilde{s}^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{12}}\epsilon^{B_{1}\ldots B_{8}}\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{5}B_{1}{\rm 1}}\ldots\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{12}B_{8}{\rm 1}}=8!\,(\rho^{\#})^{-2}\epsilon_{A_{1}\ldots A_{8}}{v}{}^{-\alpha_{1}A_{1}}_{{\rm 1}}\ldots{v}{}^{-\alpha_{4}A_{4}}_{{\rm 1}}\,s^{A_{5}\ldots A_{8}}\;,\qquad

(D.45)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\qquad\tilde{s}^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{12}}\epsilon^{q_{1}\ldots q_{8}}{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{5}q_{1}{\rm 1}}\ldots{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{12}q_{8}{\rm 1}}=8!\,(\rho^{\#})^{-2}\epsilon_{\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{8}}\bar{v}{}^{-\alpha_{1}}_{\dot{p}_{1}{\rm 1}}\ldots\bar{v}{}^{-\alpha_{4}}_{\dot{p}_{4}{\rm 1}}\,\tilde{s}_{\dot{p}_{5}\ldots\dot{p}_{8}}\;,

(D.46)

where
s^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{12}}
is defined in (
5.60
).
This is the consequence of

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{16}}{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{9}q_{1}}\ldots{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{16}q_{8}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{8}}=8!\,\epsilon_{{q}_{1}\ldots{q}_{8}}v_{\dot{q}_{1}}^{-\alpha_{1}}\ldots v_{\dot{q}_{1}}^{\alpha_{8}}\qquad

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\qquad\epsilon_{{q}_{1}\ldots{q}_{8}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{16}}{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{9}q_{1}}\ldots{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{16}q_{8}}=8!\,\epsilon_{{q}_{1}\ldots{q}_{8}}v_{\dot{q}_{1}}^{-\alpha_{1}}\ldots v_{\dot{q}_{8}}^{\alpha_{8}}\qquad

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\qquad\epsilon^{{B}_{1}\ldots{B}_{8}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{16}}\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{9}B_{1}}\ldots\bar{v}{}^{-}_{\alpha_{16}B_{8}}=8!\,\epsilon_{{B}_{1}\ldots{B}_{8}}v^{-\alpha_{1}B_{1}}\ldots v^{-\alpha_{8}B_{8}}\qquad

(D.47)

which, in its turn, follows from

{\rm det}(v_{\alpha q}^{\;-},v_{\alpha\dot{q}}^{\;+})=1\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{16}}v_{\alpha_{1}q_{1}}^{\;-}\ldots v_{\alpha_{8}q_{8}}^{\;-}v_{\alpha_{9}\dot{q}_{1}}^{\;+}\ldots v_{\alpha_{16}\dot{q}_{8}}^{\;+}=\epsilon_{{q}_{1}\ldots{q}_{8}}\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{8}}\;.

(D.48)

Line (
D.43
) is obtained by using the identity

\epsilon_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{q}_{1}\ldots\dot{q}_{4}}\bar{\tilde{{\rm s}}}{}_{\dot{p}_{1}\ldots\dot{p}_{4}}=1\;,

(D.49)

and passing to the last line we use the definition (
D.29
).

Finally the last form, (
D.44
), is obtained by using (
V.3
).

D.4
Some technicalities on type IIA scattering description with T-duality directions and Cartan forms of spinor moving frame of scattering particles

The condition (
5.67
) is equivalent to

k_{\mu}u^{\mu=}_{{\rm i}}=0=k_{\mu}u^{\mu\#}_{{\rm i}}\qquad\forall\,{\rm i}\;.

(D.50)

Considering these equations
in the gauge (
5.6
)–(
5.8
) we find that it results in the following
conditions for contractions of
k_{\mu}
with vectors from reference frame

\displaystyle k^{\#}=k_{\mu}u^{\mu\#}=0\;,\qquad

(D.51)

\displaystyle{\hbox{\mybb K}}_{{\rm i}}^{=j}k^{j}+k^{=}:=k_{\mu}u^{\mu j}{\hbox{\mybb K}}_{{\rm i}}^{=j}+k_{\mu}u^{\mu=}=0\;.\qquad

(D.52)

Eq. (
D.51
) results in identification of all
k^{j}_{{\rm i}}=k_{\mu}u^{\mu j}_{{\rm i}}
with
k^{j}=k_{\mu}u^{\mu j}

k^{j}_{{\rm i}}=k^{j}:=k_{\mu}u^{\mu j}\;,\qquad\forall\;{\rm i}\;.\qquad

(D.53)

Furthermore, (
D.52
) implies

\vec{k}{\vec{{\hbox{\mybb K}}}}_{{\rm i}^{\prime}{\rm i}}:=\vec{k}({\vec{{\hbox{\mybb K}}}}_{{\rm i}^{\prime}}-{\vec{{\hbox{\mybb K}}}}_{{\rm i}})=0

(D.54)

for any
{\rm i}
and
{\rm i}^{\prime}
.

In the gauge
(
5.6
)–(
5.8
), the Cartan form of the reference spinor moving frame are related by

\displaystyle\Omega^{=j}_{{\rm i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega^{=j}+D{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j}_{{\rm i}}-\frac{1}{2}{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j}_{{\rm i}}\,\;\Omega^{\#j^{\prime}}{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j^{\prime}}_{{\rm i}}-\frac{1}{4}\Omega^{\#j}\,|\vec{{\hbox{\mybb K}}}{}^{=}_{{\rm i}}|^{2}\;,\qquad

(D.55)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\#j}_{{\rm i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega^{\#j}\;,\qquad

(D.56)

\displaystyle\Omega^{(0)}_{{\rm i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega^{(0)}+\frac{1}{4}{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j}_{{\rm i}}\Omega^{\#j}=0\;,\qquad

(D.57)

\displaystyle\Omega^{jk}_{{\rm i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega^{jk}+{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=[j}_{{\rm i}}\Omega^{\#k]}\;,\qquad

(D.58)

where

\displaystyle D{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j}_{{\rm i}}=d{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j}_{{\rm i}}-2{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j}_{{\rm i}}\,\;\Omega^{(0)}+{\hbox{\mybb K}}^{=j^{\prime}}_{{\rm i}}\;\Omega^{j^{\prime}j}\;\qquad

(D.59)

is covariant derivative with connection composed from reference spinor moving frame.

Appendix E
Spinor moving frame approach to three point type IIA amplitudes involving D
0
–brane

E.1
Kinematics of three particle type IIA process involving D
0
-brane

Using the gauge fixing decomposition of spinor moving frame variables of massless superparticles on the reference spinor moving frame, Eqs.
(
5.4
), and also its consequence (
5.6
), we can split the above momentum conservation condition (
5.109
) on three equations. These appear as coefficients
for
v_{\alpha q}^{-}v_{\beta p}^{-}
,
v_{(\alpha|q}^{-}v_{|\beta)\dot{p}}^{+}\tilde{\gamma}{}^{i}_{q\dot{p}}
and
v_{(\alpha|\dot{q}}^{+}v_{|\beta)\dot{q}}^{+}

in the contraction of Eq. (
5.109
) with
\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}
and read

\displaystyle(l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}+l_{{\rm 2}q}^{+\underline{q}})\,(l_{{\rm 1}p}^{+\underline{q}}+l_{{\rm 2}p}^{+\underline{q}})=2(l_{{\rm 1}}^{\#}+l_{{\rm 2}}^{\#})\delta_{qp}\;,

(E.60)

\displaystyle K^{=i}_{{\rm 1}}\,(l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}+l_{{\rm 2}q}^{+\underline{q}})\,l_{{\rm 1}p}^{+\underline{q}}+K^{=i}_{{\rm 2}}\,(l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}+l_{{\rm 2}q}^{+\underline{q}})\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{+\underline{q}}=2(l_{{\rm 1}}^{\#}K^{=i}_{{\rm 1}}+l_{{\rm 2}}^{\#}K^{=i}_{{\rm 2}})\delta_{qp}\;,

(E.61)

\displaystyle\gamma^{i}_{q(\dot{q}}\tilde{\gamma}^{j}_{\dot{p})p}\left(K^{=i}_{{\rm 1}}\,K^{=j}_{{\rm 1}}\,l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 1}p}^{+\underline{q}}+K^{=i}_{{\rm 2}}\,K^{=j}_{{\rm 2}}\,l_{{\rm 2}q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{+\underline{q}}+2K^{=i}_{{\rm 1}}\,K^{=j}_{{\rm 2}}\,l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{+\underline{q}}\right)=2(l_{{\rm 1}}^{\#}(\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 1}})^{2}+l_{{\rm 2}}^{\#}(\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 2}})^{2})\delta_{qp}\;.

(E.62)

(To derive these equations we have used the constraints (
5.91
) and (
2.22
)).

As
K^{=i}_{{\rm 1}}
and
K^{=i}_{{\rm 2}}
are not restricted by momentum conservation condition (
5.109
), Eq. (
E.61
) implies

\displaystyle(l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}+l_{{\rm 2}q}^{+\underline{q}})\,l_{{\rm 1}p}^{+\underline{q}}=2l_{{\rm 1}}^{\#}\delta_{qp}\;,\qquad

\displaystyle(l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}+l_{{\rm 2}q}^{+\underline{q}})\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{+\underline{q}})=2l_{{\rm 2}}^{\#}\delta_{qp}\;.\qquad

(E.63)

The sum of this equation reproduces (
E.60
) which is hence dependent.
Furthermore any of these equations can be used to find
l_{{\rm 1}[q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 2}p]}^{+\underline{q}}=0
. Hence, as the basis of symmetric
8\times 8
matrices is given by
\delta_{qp}
and
\gamma^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}_{qp}
,
we conclude that

\displaystyle l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{+\underline{q}}=2l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#}\delta_{qp}+\frac{1}{4!}l^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}\#}\gamma^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}_{qp}

(E.64)

with some
l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#}
and
l^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}\#}=l^{[i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}]\#}
.
Then Eqs. (
E.1
) can be written in the form of

\displaystyle l_{{\rm 1}q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 1}p}^{+\underline{q}}=2(l_{{\rm 1}}^{\#}-l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#})\delta_{qp}-\frac{1}{4!}l^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}\#}\gamma^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}_{qp}\;,\qquad

(E.65)

\displaystyle l_{{\rm 2}q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm 2}p}^{+\underline{q}}=2(l_{{\rm 2}}^{\#}-l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#})\delta_{qp}-\frac{1}{4!}l^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}\#}\gamma^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}_{qp}\;.\qquad

(E.66)

Thus all
l_{{\rm i}q}^{+\underline{q}}\,l_{{\rm j}p}^{+\underline{q}}
are symmetric which implies that Eq. (
E.62
) contains
\gamma^{i}_{(q|(\dot{q}}\tilde{\gamma}^{j}_{\dot{p})|p)}=\gamma^{(i|}_{q(\dot{q}}\tilde{\gamma}^{|j)}_{\dot{p})p}
and, furthermore, that this equation reduces to the following equation involving as a parameter
the vector
K^{=i}_{{\rm 21}}=K^{=i}_{{\rm 2}}-K^{=i}_{{\rm 1}}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma^{(i}_{q(\dot{q}}\tilde{\gamma}^{j)}_{\dot{p})p}K^{=i}_{{\rm 21}}\,K^{=j}_{{\rm 21}}\left(l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#}\delta_{qp}+\frac{1}{4!}l^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}\#}\gamma^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}_{qp}\right)\equiv

(E.68)

\displaystyle\equiv-2(\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}})^{2}l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#}\delta_{\dot{q}\dot{p}}-K^{=i}_{{\rm 21}}\,K^{=j}_{{\rm 21}}\,\frac{1}{4!}l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#k_{1}...k_{4}}(\tilde{\gamma}^{(i}\gamma^{k_{1}...k_{4}}\tilde{\gamma}^{j)})_{(\dot{q}\dot{p})}\;.

Decomposing this matrix equation on the irreducible parts, we arrive at

\displaystyle l_{{\rm 12}}^{\#}\,(\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 21}})^{2}=0\;\qquad

(E.69)

\displaystyle 8{K}^{=j}_{{\rm 12}}l^{\#j[i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}{K}^{=i_{4}]}_{{\rm 12}}+l^{\#i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}(\vec{K}^{=}_{{\rm 12}})^{2}=0\;.

(E.70)
